For all
n>0
the category
Crs(Grp)_{\geq n}
is equivalent to the category
ch(Ab)_{\geq n}\cong ch(Ab)
of chain complexes in abelian groups.

Thus, for
n\geq 2
we have a functor
\mathbf{tr^{\prime}}_{n}:Crs(Grp)\to Crs(Grp)_{\geq n}
defined for a crossed complex
M
by

\mathbf{tr^{\prime}}_{n}(M)=\hbox to251.65pt{\vbox to16.65pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 125.8251pt\lower-8.3264pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

The natural chain complex morphism
f:M\to\mathbf{tr^{\prime}}_{n}(M)
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

is a morphism in
Crs(Grp)
if and only if all the actions
M_{0}\to Aut(M_{i})
are trivial for
i\geq n
. Indeed,
M
should satisfy
{}^{m_{0}}m_{n}=f_{n}(^{m_{0}}m_{n})=^{f_{0}(m_{0})}f_{n}(m_{n})=m_{n}
for all
m_{0}\in M_{0}
and
m_{n}\in M_{n}
. In particular, this condition holds if
\delta_{1}:M_{1}\to M_{0}
is a central extension, since in a crossed complex the restrictions of the actions
\delta_{1}(M_{1})\to Aut(M_{0})
are trivial.

Proposition 6.13
.

For
n\geq 2
, consider the triplet of subcategories:

(Ker(\mathbf{cot}_{n-1}),Crs(Grp)_{n-1\geq},Crs(Grp)_{\geq n})\,.

in
Crs(Grp)
. Then:

1.

the pair
(Ker(\mathbf{cot}_{n-1}),Crs(Grp)_{n-1\geq})
is CTF theory, i.e., the subcategory
Crs(Grp)_{n-1\geq}
is mono-coreflective;

2.

the pair
(Crs(Grp)_{n-1\geq},Crs(Grp)_{\geq n})
is an
\mathcal{E}
-torsion theory where
\mathcal{E}
is the class of crossed complexes
M
with all actions
M_{0}\to Aut(M_{i})
trivial for
i\geq n
;

3.

if
M
is a crossed complex with
\delta_{1}:M_{1}\to M_{0}
a crossed module central extension then
M
belongs to
\mathcal{E}
.

In particular, for
n=2
this holds for the triplet:

(Ker(\mathbf{cot}_{1}),\mathbb{X}Mod,chn(Ab)_{\geq 2})\,.

Proof.

1) From Proposition
5.4
,
\mu^{\prime}_{n-1\geq}=(Ker(\mathbf{cot}_{n-1}),Crs(Grp)_{n-1\geq})
is a torsion theory. It suffices to notice that the counit of
\mathbf{sk}_{n-1}\dashv\mathbf{tr}_{n-1}
given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}

is monic since each component is an injective morphism.

2) It is clear that the pair
(Crs(Grp)_{n-1\geq},Crs(Grp)_{\geq n})
satisfies TT1 of the definition of a
\mathcal{E}
-torsion theory. Now, let
M
be a crossed complex with trivial actions
M_{0}\to Aut(M_{i})
and consider the morphisms
\mathbf{tr}_{n-1}(M)\to M\to\mathbf{tr^{\prime}}_{n}(M)
in
Crs(Grp)
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}

recall that the morphism
M\to\mathbf{tr^{\prime}}_{n}(M)
is indeed a morphism in
Crs(Grp)
since the actions are trivial. It is a short exact sequence in
Crs(Grp)
since it is a short exact sequence as chain complexes and the forgetful functor is conservative.

3) It follows from the definition of crossed complex that if
\delta_{1}
is surjective the actions
\delta_{1}(M_{1})=M_{0}\to Aut(M_{i})
are trivial.
∎

6.3
An example of a splitting CTF theory in a semi-abelian category

In an abelian category a torsion theory
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{F})
is called splitting if the torsion subobject
t(X)
of
X
is a direct summand. In a semi-abelian category we will call a torsion theory
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{F})

splitting
if for every object
X
the associated exact sequence splits:

In
RMod
the category of modules over the ring
R
, a central idempotent element of
R
induces a splitting torsion theory
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{F})
(also called centrally splitting), and even yields a TTF theory
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{T},\mathcal{F})
. Connections of splitting torsion theories and TTF theories are studied in
[
24
]
.

The torsion theories in simplicial groups and crossed complexes presented earlier are not splitting. However, we give an example of a splitting torsion theory in a semi-abelian category.

Example 6.14
.

Let
KHopf_{coc}
the category of cocommutative Hopf alegras over the field
K
of characteristic 0. In
[
19
]
, the category
KHopf_{coc}
is proved that it is a semi-abelian category and have a torsion theory
(KLie,Grp)
where the
K
-Lie algebras are consider as the primitive Hopf
K
-algebras and
Grp
is considered as the category of group Hopf
K
-algebras. Indeed, for every
K
-algebra
H
the associated short exact sequence is given by the Cartier-Gabriel-Milnor-Moore-Kostant theorem: there is a split short exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U(L_{H})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K[G_{H}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

where
K[G_{H}]
is the group algebra of the group-like elements
G_{H}
of
H
and
U(L_{H})
is the enveloping algebra of the primitive elements
L_{H}
of
H
.

In
[
20
]
, it is proved that the funtor
\mathcal{G}:KHopf_{coc}\to Grp
that takes the group-like elements and
K[\_]:Grp\to KHopf_{coc}
yield the adjunctions
\mathcal{G}\dashv K[\_]
and
K[\_]\dashv\mathcal{G}
. As a consequence,
(KLie,Grp)
is a splitting CTF theory.
